# MediCRM — User Acceptance Test Plan

## 1. Overview

This document is a click-through User Acceptance Test (UAT) plan for MediCRM, a medical distribution CRM. It is written for a client or QA tester operating the app through a real browser — no code, API calls, or developer tools are required for the vast majority of cases (a handful of Permission-boundary cases that specifically need to prove a *server-side* rejection, not just a hidden button, call this out explicitly in their steps).

**731 test cases across 19 feature areas**, covering:
- Every page and its buttons/fields/flows (10 core feature areas)
- Cross-cutting concerns that touch every page: permissions, error handling, data integrity, and session lifecycle (4 areas)
- Deeper gaps identified during plan construction: concurrent editing, a hidden Notes field, dropdown data sourcing, mobile/responsive layout, and keyboard/accessibility (5 areas)

MediCRM has exactly **two roles**:
- **Admin** — sees and manages everything.
- **Caller** — sees and manages only their own assigned leads, orders, renewals, follow-ups, and notifications. This boundary is enforced by PostgreSQL row-level security in the database itself, not merely by hiding buttons in the UI — several test cases exist specifically to prove the *server* rejects an action a Caller shouldn't be able to take, since the corresponding button is sometimes still visible and clickable for a Caller (a UX gap noted throughout, distinct from the security boundary itself, which does hold).

This plan was produced by having independent reviewers read the actual current source code (frontend pages, backend routes, and database schema/migrations) for each feature area rather than working from assumptions — every test case is grounded in what the code actually does today. During compilation, four claims from the initial draft were found to contradict the live, running system (each reviewer worked from a single feature area and, in these four cases, cited the original `db/schema.sql` text without accounting for a later migration or an application-level fix that supersedes it) and were corrected by re-verifying the actual behavior directly against the database before publishing:
- A Caller genuinely **can** Renew their own assigned renewal (an early draft wrongly flagged this as broken).
- A Caller genuinely **cannot** soft-delete their own lead — a dedicated trigger blocks it (an early draft wrongly claimed this succeeds).
- A Caller genuinely **can** soft-delete their own user account, with no protective trigger (an early draft wrongly claimed this is blocked) — this one **is** a real, verified risk, kept in the plan as such.
- The admin-only Dashboard panels **do** refresh automatically, every 5 minutes, via the backend's own scheduler (an early draft wrongly claimed there is no refresh mechanism at all).

## 2. Test Environment & Setup

1. From the project root, run `npm run dev:all` (starts both the Vite frontend on `http://localhost:5173` and the Express backend on port 3001; the frontend proxies `/api` calls automatically — no separate configuration needed).
2. Ensure PostgreSQL is running and the `medcrm` database is freshly seeded (`db/seed.sql`) before starting a full pass — many cases quote exact counts and names from the seed data, and several cases *mutate* shared seed data (converting leads, renewing renewals, changing order stages) with no undo in the UI. Re-seed between full test cycles, or run mutating cases last within a cycle.
3. Open `http://localhost:5173` in a browser. No account creation step is needed — use the demo credentials below.

**Demo accounts:**

| Role | Email | Password | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Admin | aarav.sharma@medicrm.in | admin123 | Aarav Sharma — full access |
| Caller | sneha.iyer@medicrm.in | caller123 | Sneha Iyer — 4 assigned leads, used for all Caller-role cases |
| Caller (inactive) | kavya.reddy@medicrm.in | caller123 | Kavya Reddy — deliberately deactivated; used only for the negative-login test (login must fail with the same generic message as a wrong password) |

Today's date in this environment is **2026-07-17** — several cases (Renewals days-remaining, Calendar Today/Overdue counts) quote exact numbers computed against this date and note the formula to recompute if run later.

**Priority key:** P0 = must pass before any release (core function or security boundary); P1 = high — should pass, blocks a smooth release if broken; P2 = medium — real but non-blocking; P3 = low — edge case or polish.

## 4. Permissions Matrix — Admin vs. Caller

Enforced by PostgreSQL row-level security (RLS) on every table, independent of and in most cases *not mirrored by* the frontend UI (many Admin-only buttons render identically for a Caller and fail only when the server rejects the request). Two distinct failure shapes exist and are called out in the relevant test cases:
- **Masked "not found"** — RLS silently filters the target row out of the query, so the app's own zero-rows check returns a generic 404 ("Lead not found", "Order not found") even for a record the Caller could see moments earlier in a list.
- **Explicit permission error** — a database trigger or policy violation surfaces as a specific error toast (e.g. "callers may not soft-delete or restore leads").

| Resource | Action | Admin | Caller |
|---|---|---|---|
| Leads | View list / detail | All leads | Own assigned only |
| Leads | Create | Yes, any assignment | Yes — auto-assigned to self |
| Leads | Edit | Yes, any lead | Own only |
| Leads | Delete (soft) | Yes | **No** — blocked by a dedicated trigger even on their own leads |
| Lead medicines / comments | View / add | Any lead | Own leads only |
| Convert lead → order | — | Any lead | Own leads only |
| Schedule follow-up (from a lead) | — | Any lead | Own leads only |
| Users | View list / detail | All users | Self only |
| Users | Create | Yes — **including other Admins** (no higher role exists to restrict this since super_admin was removed) | No |
| Users | Edit | Yes, any user | Self only — and even then, role/status/employee ID are blocked by a trigger |
| Users | Delete (soft) | Yes | **Yes, even their own account** — verified, no protective trigger exists; a genuine self-lockout risk |
| Medicines catalog | View | All (active + inactive) | All (active + inactive) — read-only |
| Medicines catalog | Create / Edit / Delete / Toggle status | Yes | No |
| Orders | View | All orders | Only orders linked to their own leads |
| Orders | Create | No manual create exists for **either** role — orders are only produced by converting a lead | | 
| Orders | Advance stage / change payment status | Yes | No — masked as "Order not found" |
| Renewals | View | All | Own assigned only |
| Renewals | Renew | Yes, any | **Yes — own only** (fixed by migration 005; do not trust `db/schema.sql`'s literal text for this policy, it shows the pre-fix version) |
| Renewals | Schedule reminder | Yes, any | Yes — own only |
| Follow-ups | View | All | Own assigned only |
| Follow-ups | Mark complete / update | Yes, any | Own only |
| Notifications | View / mark read | **Own only, even for Admin** — strictly personal, no admin override | Own only |
| Dashboard | Live stat cards, lead breakdown | Scoped to all data | Scoped to own data |
| Dashboard | "Caller Performance" panel | Visible | Not shown at all |

## 5. Known Limitations

These are confirmed, code-verified behaviors that a tester should **not** log as new defects — they are called out here so the team can decide whether/when to address them, and so testers don't waste time reporting what's already known:

- **Auth/session:** no lockout or rate-limiting on repeated failed logins; the "Forgot?" link and the profile menu's "Profile"/"Settings" items are inert (no handler wired); nothing redirects an already-authenticated user away from `/login` if they navigate to it directly.
- **Dashboard:** the "Monthly Orders" chart is 100% static placeholder data, never reflecting real orders; the stat-card trend badges (+12%, -5%, etc.) are hardcoded per card and never change; Dashboard data loads once at login and is not re-fetched on navigating back to "/" (a hard refresh or re-login is needed to see updated counts); the "Today's Calls" card counts every follow-up type (call/reminder/callback) and every status, not just pending phone calls; "Converted Orders" actually counts all non-deleted orders regardless of fulfillment stage. Admin's matview-backed panels refresh every 5 minutes via the backend's own scheduler, not instantly (see Known corrections above).
- **Leads:** the "Next Follow-up" column header is wired to sort by Created Date, not the displayed Next Follow-up value; clearing Alternate Number, Doctor Name, or resetting Assigned Caller to "Unassigned" in the Edit form does not persist as blank — the frontend drops empty fields from the update payload entirely, silently preserving the prior value; there is no confirmation dialog before delete; the medicine picker matches only the catalog's exact brand name (not generic name) — a non-match falls back to free text, and a Caller attempting to add a genuinely new catalog medicine this way gets a silent 403 on the catalog write while the lead itself still saves with the free-text name.
- **Lead Detail:** the "Edit" button does not open an edit form at all — it navigates back to the Leads list; a lead's intake Notes are collected nowhere in the UI and displayed nowhere in the UI, even though the field exists end-to-end in the database and API (see the dedicated Lead Notes section below); "Convert to Order" is gated only on status not already being "Converted" — a "Closed" lead can still be converted.
- **Users:** no confirmation dialog before delete or status toggle; the page applies no client-side role gating at all (only the sidebar link is hidden for Callers); new users get a fixed default password never entered into or shown by the UI.
- **Medicines:** leaving Generic Name blank on create defaults it server-side to the Medicine Name; clearing Generic Name to blank on an existing medicine does not clear it, because the frontend omits empty fields from the update payload; no confirmation dialog before delete or status toggle.
- **Orders:** no per-record deep-link URL — only a modal opened from the table.
- **Renewals:** "Call customer" and "View details" row actions have no handler at all (permanently inert); neither Renew nor Schedule Reminder shows a success toast (only failures toast); Renew has no confirmation dialog despite being irreversible; the page has no per-record URL; the client-side search's caller match compares against the raw user ID, not the display name.
- **Calendar:** with a freshly-seeded database on 2026-07-17, no follow-up is dated exactly "today" for any account, so the Today tile/panel start empty by design, not by bug.
- **Notifications:** the header's global search box updates local state only and is **not wired to any real search** — do not expect it to return results; notifications are fetched once at login with no polling, so new notifications never appear until refresh/re-login; the bell shows a pulsing dot, not a numeric badge (the count only appears inside the open dropdown).
- **Global lookups endpoint:** the backend exposes `GET /api/lookups`, but the frontend never calls it — Lead Source and Dosage Form dropdown options are hardcoded arrays in the frontend instead (they match the seeded data, so this has no visible effect today, but the two sources could drift apart in the future without anyone noticing via the UI).
- **Assigned Caller dropdown:** does not filter out deactivated callers — a deactivated caller still appears as a selectable option when assigning a lead.
- **Data integrity:** none of the Add/Edit modals (Leads, Users, Medicines) disable or spinner their Save button while a request is in flight, allowing an accidental double-submit; there is no optimistic-concurrency protection anywhere — two sessions editing the same Lead/User/Medicine record at the same time will silently overwrite each other's changes to unrelated fields, because every Edit modal resubmits the *entire* record snapshot on save, not a diff. This is a genuine data-integrity risk for real multi-user usage, detailed fully in its own section below.
- **Accessibility:** form labels are not programmatically associated with their inputs (no `htmlFor`/`id` pairing) across every Add/Edit modal; the modal component does not trap keyboard focus inside itself while open.

## 6. Detailed Test Cases by Area

## Authentication & Session

Covers the MediCRM login page (http://localhost:5173/login) end to end — manual credential entry, the one-click demo-role buttons, every field/button on the page, and error states — plus session lifecycle: token persistence in localStorage across hard refreshes, the forced redirect-and-toast flow on an expired/invalid session, logout (including that it wipes the entire app state, not just the user), and the Admin/Caller permission boundary as it applies to this area (sidebar visibility of "User Management" and, more importantly, that a Caller who directly navigates to /users is not blocked by the frontend router but is limited by Postgres row-level security to seeing only their own user record). Test cases are grounded in the current source (src/pages/Login.tsx, src/context/AppContext.tsx, src/App.tsx, src/components/layout/TopNav.tsx and Sidebar.tsx, src/api/client.ts, server/src/auth.ts, and the RLS policies in db/schema.sql). Known limitations testers should be aware of going in: there is no lockout/rate-limiting on repeated failed logins, the "Forgot?" link and the profile dropdown's "Profile"/"Settings" items are currently inert (no handler wired up), and nothing redirects an already-authenticated user away from /login if they navigate to it directly. Full CRUD testing of the Users page itself belongs to a separate "User Management" UAT section — only the auth/session-relevant boundary (what a Caller's browser can reach and what data comes back) is covered here.

## Dashboard

Covers the Dashboard landing page (route "/") for both Admin and Caller roles: the five live stat cards (Total Leads, Today's Calls, Pending Follow-ups, Converted Orders, Renewals Due), the "Lead Status Breakdown" bar chart, the static "Monthly Orders" placeholder chart, the "Recent Leads" table (6 newest leads), and the admin-only "Caller Performance" panel. Test cases were grounded in src/pages/Dashboard.tsx, server/src/routes/misc.ts, and the RLS policies in db/schema.sql. Known, code-confirmed limitations to keep in mind while testing: (1) "Monthly Orders" is 100% static placeholder data (MONTHLY_ORDERS_DATA constant) — it never reflects real orders, so no live-data assertions should be made against it; (2) the stat-card trend badges (e.g. "+12%", "-5%") are hardcoded per card, not computed from any data, so they never change; (3) Dashboard data is fetched once via loadAll() at login/app-boot and is not re-fetched when navigating to "/" — a hard refresh (or re-login) is required to see updated counts after data changes elsewhere; (4) for Admin, "Lead Status Breakdown" and "Caller Performance" read from materialized views (mv_lead_status_breakdown, mv_caller_performance) which are refreshed on a 5-minute interval by the Node backend's own scheduler (server/src/scheduler.ts) rather than instantly on every write — so a very recent change can lag up to ~5 minutes behind, not indefinitely (there IS an active refresh mechanism, it just is not instant; db/VERIFICATION.md's note about pg_cron not being installed refers to a database-level scheduler that was never needed once the Node-side one was built) — a Caller's own breakdown is always computed live from the leads table so is unaffected; (5) the "Today's Calls" card counts every follow_up (type call, reminder, or callback; status pending, completed, or missed) scheduled for today — it is not restricted to phone calls or to still-pending items, so the label may overcount versus tester expectation; (6) "Converted Orders" is actually a count of all non-deleted orders regardless of fulfillment stage (lead/confirmed/medicine_prepared/packed/shipped/delivered) — it is not filtered to any specific "converted" stage. Row-level security is enforced at the database layer (FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY on leads/orders/follow_ups/renewals/users), so a Caller's stat cards, breakdown, and Recent Leads are scoped to their own assigned records by the database itself, not merely hidden in the UI.

Immediately after the change, the old numbers may still show; within 5 minutes (or after restarting the backend, which refreshes once on boot) they must reflect the new data. ## Leads — List & CRUD

Covers the Leads list page (src/pages/Leads.tsx) and its backing API (server/src/routes/leads.ts): search, status-tab filtering, column sorting, the Add/Edit modal (all fields, multi-medicine picker with catalog-match/free-text fallback), soft delete, and the Admin/Caller permission boundary enforced by Postgres row-level security. Grounded directly in the current source, including two behaviors testers should be aware of going in: (1) the "Next Follow-up" column header is wired to sort by Created Date, not by the Next Follow-up value it displays; (2) in the Edit modal, clearing Alternate Number, Doctor Name, or setting Assigned Caller back to "Unassigned" does not persist as blank/unassigned — the frontend collapses an empty string to `undefined` before sending the PATCH, so the field is omitted from the request entirely and the prior value silently survives. Delete has no confirmation prompt of any kind. Lead Detail (view page) and Convert-to-Order are separate feature areas and are only touched here where they reveal List/CRUD behavior (e.g. "Lead not found" after a caller navigates to a lead they don't own).

| ID | Title | Role | Type | Priority | Preconditions | Steps | Expected Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LEADS-01 | Golden path: view the Leads list | Admin | Functional | P0 | Logged in as aarav.sharma@medicrm.in / admin123. At least one lead exists. | 1. Navigate to http://localhost:5173 and log in as Admin.<br>2. Click 'Leads' in the navigation.<br>3. Observe the page. | Page header reads 'Lead Management' with description '<N> total leads' where N equals the total number of non-deleted leads. A table lists all leads with columns Customer Name, Mobile, Medicines, Priority, Status, Assigned To, Next Follow-up, Actions. An 'Add Lead' button with a plus icon sits in the header. A search box with placeholder 'Search by name, mobile, or medicine...' and a row of status tabs (All, New, Contacted, Follow-up, Interested, Converted) sit above the table. |

## Lead Detail Page

Covers the single-lead detail view at /leads/:id — header (customer name, mobile, status badge, priority badge, Edit / Convert to Order / Schedule Follow-up buttons), the Customer Information, Medicines Required, Follow-up Details and Add Comment cards, the Activity Timeline, and the Admin/Caller permission boundary enforced by row-level security at the database layer (not just hidden in the UI). Test data references the seeded MediCRM demo dataset: Sneha Iyer (caller) has exactly 4 assigned leads — two for "Ramesh Gupta" (one already Converted, one Follow-up Pending), "Rajesh Patel" (Closed), and "Deepa Nambiar" (Call Back Later) — and Vikram Singh owns "Sunita Verma", useful as a lead Sneha should NOT be able to see. Known gaps surfaced while reading the source and worth flagging to the team: the page's "Edit" button does not open an edit form for the current lead at all (it just navigates back to the Leads list), a lead's intake Notes are never displayed anywhere on this page, the "No activity recorded yet" empty state appears unreachable in practice (every lead gets a "Lead created" activity automatically), and "Convert to Order" is gated only on status being exactly "Converted" — a "Closed" lead can still be converted. Several permission tests require two simultaneous browser sessions (e.g. an incognito window) to simulate an Admin changing a lead out from under a Caller who has the page open; these are called out explicitly in their preconditions.

| ID | Title | Role | Type | Priority | Preconditions | Steps | Expected Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LEADDTL-01 | Admin opens a lead from the Leads list and sees full detail | Admin | Functional | P0 |  | 1. Log in as aarav.sharma@medicrm.in / admin123.<br>2. Click "Leads" in the left sidebar.<br>3. Click anywhere on the row for "Ramesh Gupta" (or any lead) in the table. | The browser navigates to that lead's detail page. The header shows the customer's name and mobile number, a status badge, and a priority badge (e.g. "Medium Priority"). Below the header, a Customer Information card, a Medicines Required card, a Follow-up Details card and an Add Comment card appear on the left, and an Activity Timeline card appears on the right. "Edit" and "Schedule Follow-up" buttons are visible top-right, plus "Convert to Order" if the lead is not already Converted. |

## Users Management (Admin feature)

Covers src/pages/Users.tsx (the /users route) end to end: the Admin golden path (view, search, create, edit, toggle status, delete), every field/button on the Create/Edit modal with its validation, empty/zero-result states, and the Admin/Caller permission boundary for this page. Grounded in the actual current code: server/src/routes/users.ts, db/schema.sql + db/migrations/004_remove_super_admin.sql (RLS policies + the trg_prevent_privilege_escalation trigger), server/src/errors.ts (exact toast text for constraint violations), server/src/auth.ts and db/migrations/006_auth_helpers.sql (login/session status checks), and src/context/AppContext.tsx (how UPDATE_USER does/doesn't touch currentUser). Known limitations testers should be aware of going in: there is no confirmation dialog before delete or before a status toggle; Users.tsx has no client-side role gating at all (the "Add User" button and every row's edit/delete icons render for a Caller too — only the sidebar link and server-side RLS/trigger checks stand between a Caller and these actions); a caller directly hitting the Delete action on her own row is a genuine open question given the code (the privilege-escalation trigger blocks role/status/employee_id changes for callers but does not appear to guard the soft-delete's deleted_at column, and the users_update RLS policy admits a caller acting on her own id) — flagged below as a case to verify carefully rather than assumed either way. New users are given a fixed default password server-side that is never entered into or surfaced by the UI.

| ID | Title | Role | Type | Priority | Preconditions | Steps | Expected Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

In the left sidebar, under the "Administration" section, click "User Management".<br>3. Observe the page that loads. | The page heading reads "User Management" with a subtitle in the form "<N> team members · <N> active" where N matches the number of rows in the table. The table lists every user in the system (not just the admin's own account) with columns User, Employee ID, Phone, Role, Leads, Status, Last Login, and a final unlabeled column holding a pencil (edit) and trash (delete) icon per row. An "Add User" button with a plus icon sits in the top-right of the page header, and a search box with placeholder "Search by name, email, or employee ID..." sits above the table. |

As Caller at /users, notice that the "Add User" button in the top-right is present and enabled — identical to what Admin sees.<br>2. Click "Add User" and fill in every field with valid values for a hypothetical new user.<br>3. ## Medicines Catalog

Covers the Medicines catalog page (src/pages/Medicines.tsx) at /medicines: viewing the catalog table, searching by medicine or generic name, adding/editing medicines via the modal, toggling Active/Inactive status, and deleting medicines. Read access to the catalog is available to both Admin and Caller (the "Medicines" sidebar link is not admin-gated and the database's products_select policy allows any authenticated role to read all rows, active or inactive). However, writes (create/update/delete) are restricted to Admin only by Postgres row-level security policies on the products table — critically, this restriction is NOT mirrored in the UI: the "Add Medicine" button, the Edit/Delete icons, and the clickable status badge are rendered identically for Callers, with no disabled state or hidden control. A Caller who clicks any of these will see the request reach the server and fail there (surfaced as an error toast), not be blocked client-side. Testers should be aware of two non-obvious, code-verified quirks: (1) leaving "Generic Name" blank when adding a medicine does NOT store it as blank — the backend defaults it to the same value as the Medicine Name; (2) clearing "Generic Name" to blank on an existing medicine and saving does NOT clear it in the database, because the frontend drops empty fields from the update payload entirely, so the prior value silently survives. Delete and the status-toggle badge both act immediately with no confirmation dialog. Destructive test cases below recommend using a medicine created specifically for the test rather than the seeded catalog rows (Glycomet, Amlopres, Atorva, Lantus, Eltroxin, Azithral), since those seeded products are referenced by other UAT areas (Leads, Orders).

| ID | Title | Role | Type | Priority | Preconditions | Steps | Expected Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## Orders

Covers the Orders pipeline page (src/pages/Orders.tsx) at /orders: the six stage pipeline/filter cards (Lead/Confirmed/Medicine Prepared/Packed/Shipped/Delivered), the tab bar + search, the orders table, and the order detail modal (stage stepper, medicines table, payment-status dropdown, Advance-to-next-stage action). Expected values are grounded in the current db/seed.sql dataset: Admin (Aarav Sharma) sees all 6 seed orders (ORD-2026-0001..0006, header reads "6 total orders"); Caller Sneha Iyer is the assigned_caller on lead 40000000-...-0001 only, so per the orders_select RLS policy (order visible only if its lead_id's assigned_caller_id matches the caller) she sees exactly one order, ORD-2026-0001 (Ramesh Gupta, Delivered, Paid, total Rs.5,130.00, header reads "1 total orders") -- every other seed order either belongs to a different caller's lead or has no lead_id at all and is invisible to any caller. Known limitations for testers: (1) several cases here mutate shared seed data (stage advances, payment-status changes) with no "undo" in the UI -- reseed the database between test cycles or run mutating cases last; (2) Orders has no per-record deep-link URL (only a modal opened from the table), so the "caller shouldn't see this record via direct URL" check is necessarily done via the list route and search, not a blocked individual page; (3) the demo Caller account's only visible order is already in the terminal Delivered stage, so exercising the caller-blocked "advance stage" write requires the precondition of first having an Admin convert one of Sneha's own not-yet-converted leads into a fresh order (documented in the relevant case) -- the caller-blocked payment-status write needs no such setup and can be run directly against ORD-2026-0001.

## Renewals

Covers the Renewals page (src/pages/Renewals.tsx) reached via the sidebar "Renewals" link at /renewals: the 4 clickable summary cards (Upcoming/Due Today/Overdue/Renewed) that double as status filters, the tab bar (All/Upcoming/Due Today/Overdue/Renewed) with live counts, the "Search renewals..." box, the 9-column table, and the 4 per-row action icons (Call customer / Schedule reminder / Renew order / View details). Test cases are grounded directly in the current source (Renewals.tsx, server/src/routes/renewals.ts, db/schema.sql RLS policies, src/pages/Calendar.tsx, src/types/index.ts) and current seed data, with today's date taken as 2026-07-17 per the environment. Known limitations testers should be aware of going in: (1) "Call customer" and "View details" are rendered but have no onClick handler at all in the current build — they are inert; (2) neither Renew nor Schedule reminder shows a success toast (only failures toast, in red, top-right, auto-dismissing after ~6s), and Renew has no confirmation dialog before its irreversible action; (3) the Renewals page has no per-record URL — "View details" cannot deep-link because no route exists; (4) the client-side search's "assignedCaller" match compares against the caller's raw user ID rather than the display name, so searching a caller's actual name will not match on that field; (5) note for anyone reading db/schema.sql directly: that file shows the ORIGINAL renewals_update policy (admin-only) — migration 005_lead_medicines.sql supersedes it with a policy that also allows the assigned caller to update their own renewal, which is the live, correct behavior verified end-to-end (both via automated API tests and manual browser testing) during backend development. Do not test against schema.sql's literal text for this table. Because renewal status (Upcoming/Due Today/Overdue/Renewed) is computed live from today's date vs. each renewal's stored dates, exact counts/statuses quoted from seed data (e.g. Sneha Iyer's two renewals both being "Upcoming") are accurate as of 2026-07-17 and should be re-derived if the environment's clock has moved on significantly before testing.

## Calendar & Follow-ups

Covers src/pages/Calendar.tsx (route /calendar, available to both roles in the sidebar under "Main" with no adminOnly gate) end to end: the Daily/Weekly/Monthly view switcher, Today/prev/next navigation, the real-date "today" highlight, the three Summary tiles (Today, Upcoming (7d), Overdue), the monthly grid's day-detail panel, the "Today's Follow-ups" sidebar widget, every "Mark Complete"/"Complete" entry point, the leadId-gated "View Lead" button, and the Renewals-to-Calendar integration ("Schedule reminder" creates a today-dated follow-up). Backend logic was verified against server/src/routes/followUps.ts and db/schema.sql: follow_ups RLS (follow_ups_select/_update policies) restricts Callers to rows where assigned_caller_id = their own user id while Admins (is_admin_or_above()) see and can update every row, and a BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE trigger keeps a follow-up's assigned_caller_id in sync with its parent lead/renewal (so reassigning a lead's caller silently moves the follow-up's visibility to the new caller too). Test data and expected counts assume the unmodified demo seed and the environment's current date being 2026-07-17 — with that seed, no follow-up in the database is dated exactly "today," so the "Today" tile and "Today's Follow-ups" panel start in their empty state for every account; if the suite is run on a different date or against changed data, recompute Overdue/Upcoming(7d)/Today per the rules stated in the relevant test's expected result rather than assuming the literal numbers given. Sneha Iyer (caller) owns exactly 4 follow-ups (2 completed for Ramesh Gupta/Rajesh Patel in June, 1 upcoming reminder for Ramesh Gupta on 2026-07-20 with a lead, 1 far-future renewal-sourced callback for Deepa Nambiar on 2026-08-25 with no lead); the remaining 4 seeded follow-ups (Sunita Verma, Anil Kumar - missed/overdue, Suresh Rao - renewal-sourced/overdue, Meena Joshi) belong to other callers and must never appear in Sneha's Calendar in any view. A few cases require Admin to use the Renewals page's "Schedule reminder" action or the Leads edit form's "Assigned Caller" field as setup — these are noted explicitly since they are the only ways to generate fresh, today-dated, or reassigned follow-up data through the UI.

## Notifications

Covers the bell-icon notification dropdown in the shared header (src/components/layout/TopNav.tsx), which is the only surface for notifications in MediCRM — there is no dedicated Notifications page/route. Grounded in the actual source and seed data: the bell shows a small red pulsing dot (not a numeric badge) when unreadCount > 0; the numeric count only appears inside the dropdown as an "{n} new" pill next to the "Notifications" header. The list shows at most the 10 most-recent notifications (server returns up to 50, sliced to 10 client-side) with no pagination/"view all". Clicking a row calls PATCH /notifications/:id/read and marks it read locally; it does not navigate anywhere and does not close the dropdown. Notifications are fetched once at login/boot (via loadAll) with no polling or websocket, so newly created notifications never appear until the page is refreshed or the user re-logs in. Known limitation: the header's global search box updates local state only and is not wired to any real search — tests treat this as expected behavior, not a defect. Seed data gives exactly one usable unread-notification scenario (Sneha Iyer, 1 unread "info"-type notification) and one zero-notification scenario (Aarav Sharma); several edge cases (>10 notifications, multiple unread for one account, non-"info" type-dot colors, long-title truncation) require additional test data beyond the standard demo accounts and are flagged with that precondition rather than omitted. The third demo account (Kavya Reddy) is inactive and used only for the negative login test, since MediCRM deliberately returns the same generic "Invalid email or password" message for wrong password, inactive account, and nonexistent email alike.

Run this before NOTIF-02 (which marks it read), or reset the database first. | 1. Go to http://localhost:5173<br>2. On the Sign in page, either click the 'Caller' quick-login card (sneha.iyer@medicrm.in) or type sneha.iyer@medicrm.in into 'Email address' and caller123 into 'Password' and click 'Sign in'<br>3. Once the dashboard loads, look at the bell icon in the top-right of the header, before clicking it<br>4. Click the bell icon | Before clicking: the bell icon shows a small red pulsing dot in its top-right corner (indicating at least one unread notification). After clicking: a panel titled 'Notifications' opens below the bell, showing a '1 new' pill next to the title, and exactly one row: title 'New Lead Assigned', message 'Lead for Ramesh Gupta (Atorvastatin 10mg) has been assigned to you.', a blue dot to the left of the text, a light blue-tinted row background (indicating unread), and a timestamp on the right in the form 'Xd ago' (approximately '15d ago' given the seed date of 2 Jul 2026 — value should match actual elapsed days from today). |

## Permissions & Row-Level Security Matrix

Covers the Admin-vs-Caller capability matrix for every resource in MediCRM (Leads, Lead Medicines, Lead Activities, Users, Medicines/Products, Orders, Renewals, Follow-ups, Notifications, Dashboard). Grounded in the actual code: authorization is enforced almost entirely by PostgreSQL row-level security (RLS) policies, not by role checks in the Express routes or by hiding buttons in the React UI — most pages (Users, Medicines, Leads, Orders, Renewals) render their Add/Edit/Delete controls identically for both roles with no client-side role gate, so the only real boundary is the database's rejection of the write. Two distinct failure shapes exist and testers must tell them apart: (1) a masked "not found" (RLS silently filters the row out of an UPDATE/DELETE's WHERE clause, so the app's own `rowCount === 0` check throws a generic "X not found" 404 — this happens even for a record the caller could see a moment earlier, e.g. Orders); and (2) an explicit permission error (a Postgres 42501 "row-level security policy" violation, or a P0001 RAISE EXCEPTION from a trigger/function such as `prevent_privilege_escalation` or `convert_lead_to_order`, both surfaced as a red toast). Several tests require using the browser's own Developer Tools console to fire a request directly (grabbing a record id belonging to another caller from the Admin session first) — this is explicitly called out wherever needed, per the requirement to prove the server rejects the action rather than merely checking a button is hidden. Known gaps found while reading the code and included as test cases rather than assumptions: the User Management and Medicines pages apply zero client-side role restriction to their action buttons (a Caller who navigates to /users directly gets a fully "live-looking" table with Add/Edit/Delete controls that all fail server-side); Leads and Users "Delete" buttons actually perform a soft-delete via the resource's UPDATE policy, not the (unused) admin-only DELETE policy — so a Caller genuinely can delete their own lead, and can even soft-delete their own user account, with no confirmation dialog anywhere in the app; and Orders has no caller-ownership branch at all in its update policy, so a Caller can view their own order but any write to it is masked as "Order not found."

Read the console output, then refresh the Leads page | The request fails with a database permission error (the new row violates the leads table's row-level security policy) because Sneha's own WITH CHECK clause requires the lead stay assigned to herself. | LEAD-08 | Caller CANNOT delete (soft-delete) her own lead — blocked by a lifecycle trigger, not just RLS | Caller | Permission | P0 | Logged in as Sneha Iyer, viewing one of her own leads in the Leads list. | 1. Click the Delete (trash) icon on any of her own leads.<br>2. Observe the result. | The delete FAILS with an error toast — verified directly against the database: a dedicated trigger (prevent_caller_lead_lifecycle_changes) raises "callers may not soft-delete or restore leads" for ANY caller attempting to change a lead's deleted_at column, regardless of whether the lead is their own. This is a stronger guarantee than row-level security alone (RLS's leads_update policy would otherwise permit a caller to update their own row) — the trigger closes that gap specifically for the delete/restore lifecycle. Confirmed empirically and consistent with LEADS-43 in the Leads — List & CRUD section; do not confuse this with USER-11 below, where the equivalent protection does NOT exist for a caller deleting their own user account. |

## Error Handling & Input Validation

Covers MediCRM's global error-surfacing mechanisms (the toast notification system in src/components/ui/Toast.tsx and src/lib/toast.ts, the React ErrorBoundary, and the offline/session-expiry handling in src/api/client.ts) together with field-level validation messages mapped in server/src/errors.ts (mobile, pincode, alternate-number/phone format, negative unit price, duplicate employee ID/email) and permission-boundary error behavior driven by Postgres RLS policies and triggers in db/schema.sql plus db/migrations/001-009. Grounding notes/limitations found while reading the code: (1) migration 001 normalizes leads.mobile/customers.primary_mobile (strips spaces/dashes, unwraps a +91 prefix or leading trunk 0) before the strict 10-digit/6-9 CHECK runs, so nicely formatted numbers are accepted, not just bare 10-digit strings; (2) the phone/alternate-number CHECK constraints were widened by migration 001 to 7-20 characters, matching the error copy in errors.ts exactly (no discrepancy); (3) migration 001 also made order_items.product_id nullable, so converting a lead with an uncatalogued (free-text) medicine works rather than crashing; (4) medicine SKUs are server-auto-generated (MED-00001...) with no SKU field anywhere in the UI, so the "duplicate SKU" constraint message in errors.ts cannot actually be triggered through the browser app today; (5) the Users and Medicines pages perform no client-side role gating at all (only the sidebar link is hidden for callers), so several permission-boundary cases here rely on RLS/triggers alone, exactly as the task asked to verify; (6) none of the Add/Edit modals (Leads, Users, Medicines) disable or spinner their Save button while the request is in flight, enabling double-submit races; (7) reproducing the ErrorBoundary crash screen and true session-token-expiry as a black-box click-through tester requires developer/DB assistance (noted in the relevant cases) since no normal user action is known to throw during render or to force-expire a token on demand.

The rest of the page stays fully interactive while it is showing. After about 6 seconds it disappears on its own. |

| EH-37 | Caller CAN delete her own user account — a real, verified self-lockout risk | Caller | Permission | P0 | Logged in as sneha.iyer@medicrm.in, on /users (reached by direct URL — the sidebar hides this page for Callers, but the route itself has no guard). | 1. Click the trash/delete icon on your own row. | The delete SUCCEEDS with no confirmation prompt — verified directly against the database: the users_update RLS policy permits id = app_current_user_id(), and (unlike leads, which has a dedicated trigger blocking a caller from touching deleted_at) no equivalent trigger protects users.deleted_at. Her row disappears from the table and her session is subsequently logged out with the session-expired message on her next action. This is a genuine, code-and-database-verified self-lockout risk with no undo path in the UI — consistent with USER-11 in the Permissions & RLS Matrix section — log it as a defect for product/security review, do not treat it as an expected "not found" rejection. |

## Data Integrity & Server-Computed Fields

Covers every field in MediCRM that the server/database computes and the UI must only ever display, never let a user type directly: a user's "Leads" (assigned_leads_count, trigger-maintained on the leads table), an order's Total (total_amount, incrementally recomputed from order_items on every insert/update/delete), a renewal's "Days Left" and Status badge (both derived at query time by renewals_view/compute_renewal_status from renewal_date/expiry_date/renewed_at, never stored), the order number (ORD-YYYY-NNNN via generate_order_number()/order_number_seq), and the medicine SKU (MED-NNNNN via product_sku_seq). Grounded by reading db/schema.sql, db/migrations/001_app_reconciliation.sql, db/migrations/005_lead_medicines.sql, db/migrations/004/009, server/src/routes/*.ts, server/src/errors.ts, and the actual page source under src/pages. Notable, verified-in-code facts that shape several cases below: (1) the medicine SKU is never returned by the API and never rendered anywhere in the UI (serializeMedicine in server/src/serializers.ts omits it) — a human tester cannot directly read a SKU value from the app, only confirm its side effects (no collisions, no errors); (2) there is no "Create Renewal" control anywhere in the UI — renewals only exist from seed data and can only be marked Renewed or have a reminder scheduled; (3) a lead's per-medicine "Days" value never becomes the converted order line's Quantity (convert_lead_to_order hardcodes quantity to 1 per medicine, per migration 005's own comment); (4) converting a lead whose medicine was never matched to the catalog (only reachable in practice as a Caller, since an Admin's inline "Add as new medicine" always succeeds) produces a ₹0.00 order line, silently; (5) orders_update RLS is admin-only with no lead-ownership carve-out, so a Caller viewing their own order can never advance its stage or change payment status; (6) an order whose lead_id is NULL (e.g. seeded ORD-2026-0005) is invisible to every Caller, even one who owns other leads for the same customer; (7) two icon buttons on the Renewals row ("Call customer", "View details") have no onClick handler at all and are permanently inert. Several cases mutate seed data (reassigning leads, converting leads to orders, marking renewals renewed) — expected counts assume the database is freshly seeded (db/seed.sql) before this suite runs, or that cases execute in the given order once. REN-01's concrete Days-Left/Status numbers are computed against the environment's stated current date (2026-07-17); the underlying formula is given so testers can recompute if run later. Demo accounts used: Admin aarav.sharma@medicrm.in / admin123 (Aarav Sharma); Caller sneha.iyer@medicrm.in / caller123 (Sneha Iyer, 4 assigned leads, confirmed against seed data).

This is expected, not a bug: Status is driven by whether the reminder ("Renewal Date") has arrived, while Days Left is driven purely by the expiry date — the two can diverge whenever the reminder date is reached well before the medicine actually runs out. |

## Session Lifecycle & Multi-User Hygiene

Covers establishing a session (manual and one-click demo login), ending it (Sign out), persistence across refresh/new tabs, server-side invalidation (account deactivation, tampered tokens), and — because MediCRM enforces role scoping via Postgres RLS rather than only hiding UI — the boundary cases where a Caller's browser session touches Admin-only surfaces (direct URL nav to /users and to another caller's lead). Source verified: src/context/AppContext.tsx, src/api/client.ts, src/api/auth.ts, src/pages/Login.tsx, src/App.tsx, src/components/layout/{TopNav,Sidebar,Layout}.tsx, src/pages/{Users,LeadDetailPage,Dashboard}.tsx, server/src/{auth.ts,app.ts,errors.ts,db.ts,routes/{users.ts,misc.ts}}, db/schema.sql, db/seed.sql. Known limitations testers should be aware of: (1) the token TTL is a config value (24h default) and is not practically exercisable within a UAT session, so pure time-based expiry is out of scope here; (2) the app stores its auth token in localStorage with no cross-tab sync (`storage` event), so two tabs of the SAME browser share one session and "last login wins" — several cases below exist specifically to make that visible to testers rather than to imply it's necessarily a defect; (3) /login has no guard redirecting an already-authenticated user away, and /users has no client-side role gate (only the sidebar link is hidden for Callers) — both are intentional per the RLS-first design, verified against db/schema.sql's users_select/users_update policies and the trg_prevent_privilege_escalation trigger, and are covered as permission-boundary cases rather than assumed bugs. Seed data used for exact expected counts: 8 total users (3 admin, 5 caller, Kavya Reddy inactive) → Admin's User Management header reads "8 team members · 7 active"; a Caller viewing the same page (RLS-filtered to their own row) reads "1 team members · 1 active".

## Concurrent Editing / Optimistic Concurrency (Leads, Users, Medicines)

CODE VERIFICATION FIRST: I read the full PATCH handlers in server/src/routes/leads.ts, users.ts, and medicines.ts, plus the corresponding frontend Edit modals in src/pages/Leads.tsx, Users.tsx, and Medicines.tsx, and the shared API client (src/api/client.ts) and errors module (server/src/errors.ts). Confirmed finding: NONE of the three PATCH routes implement any optimistic-concurrency check. There is no version column, no updated_at comparison, no ETag/If-Match header, and no 409 response path for a stale write — the only place ApiError.conflict()/409 is ever raised is for Postgres unique-constraint violations (duplicate email, employee ID, SKU, etc. via PG_CODE_STATUS['23505']), which is unrelated to concurrent-edit detection. Each PATCH handler builds a dynamic SQL SET clause purely from whatever keys are present in the request body and blindly runs `UPDATE ... WHERE id = $1 AND deleted_at IS NULL`, with no check against the row's prior state. Worse than plain per-field last-write-wins: the frontend Edit modals (openEdit in Leads.tsx/Users.tsx/Medicines.tsx) copy the ENTIRE record into local React form state when the modal opens, and handleSubmit always POSTs/PATCHes that full snapshot back — every editable field, not a diff of only what the user touched. So a second browser session that had the modal open before the first session's save will, on its own save, resubmit its own stale copy of every other field, silently reverting any change the first session made to those fields. This is confirmed literally in code: Leads.tsx line ~273 sends the full payload object (customerName, mobile, address, city, state, pincode, doctorName, leadSource, priority, assignedCaller) on every save; Users.tsx line ~65 sends `{ ...form }` (name, employeeId, phone, email, role); Medicines.tsx line ~91 sends the full `updates` object (name, genericName, dosageForm, unitPrice). RLS policies (db/schema.sql, leads_update policy) are row-level only — no column-level grants — so an Admin and the assigned Caller both have full field-level write access to the same lead row, meaning the same overwrite bug reproduces identically across roles. Finally, there is no client-side cache layer: AppContext's loadAll() re-fetches all lists fresh from the API on login/refresh (src/context/AppContext.tsx), so a page reload always reflects whatever is truly persisted in the database — it will surface the data loss rather than mask it with a stale cached copy. Net conclusion for the team: this is a genuine, verified data-integrity risk — two legitimately-permitted users editing the same record's ordinary fields at the same time will silently lose one user's change with zero warning, zero conflict UI, and zero server-side rejection, in Leads, Users, and Medicines alike.

Both sessions must open the Edit modal for the SAME lead before either one saves, and Browser B's modal must remain open and unrefreshed while Browser A saves. | 1. In Browser A, log in as Admin (aarav.sharma@medicrm.in / admin123) and open the Leads page.<br>2. Pick any existing lead and note its current Customer Name and City values.<br>3. In Browser A, click the pencil (Edit) icon on that lead to open its Edit modal.<br>4. Verify Browser A's modal closes and the Leads table shows the new Customer Name for that lead.<br>7. Switch to Browser B WITHOUT refreshing or reopening its modal (it still shows the ORIGINAL Customer Name from before Browser A's save). Because Browser B's form still held the pre-edit Customer Name in its local state, its Save request resubmits that stale Customer Name alongside the new City — the backend has no version/updated_at check and simply overwrites every field present in the request. Result: the lead's Customer Name silently reverts to the ORIGINAL value (Browser A's edit is lost), while City is updated to 'Pune'. After refresh (step 8), both Browser A and Browser B show Priority = Low, confirming that whichever session saved last silently won and the other user's change vanished without any notification to either party. Both Browser A and Browser B will consistently show the reverted-to-original Customer Name and the new City after reload (steps 3-4), and a brand-new modal open (step 5) will show the same values — proving the data loss from CONCUR-01 is a real, permanently persisted server-side overwrite, not a rendering or caching artifact that a refresh would 'fix'. The frontend's emitToast error handling (src/pages/Leads.tsx, Users.tsx, Medicines.tsx) only fires on a thrown ApiError from a failed request; a successful-but-conflicting overwrite is indistinguishable from a normal successful save, so no toast, dialog, inline warning, or disabled button ever appears in any of the three modals for this scenario. Testers should confirm this negative result holds in all three modules: the save silently 'succeeds' every time, regardless of whether it clobbers another user's change. |

## Lead Notes Field — Add/Edit Lead UI and Lead Detail Display

Source review of src/pages/Leads.tsx (Add/Edit Lead modal, inline in the same file — there is no separate LeadFormModal component), src/pages/LeadDetailPage.tsx, server/src/routes/leads.ts, server/src/serializers.ts, and db/schema.sql/db/seed.sql definitively establishes: (a) the Add Lead and Edit Lead popups expose exactly 12 fields — Customer Name, Mobile Number, Alternate Number, Address, City, State, Pincode, Medicines Required (repeatable name+days rows), Doctor Name (optional), Lead Source, Priority, and Assigned Caller — with no Notes/Comments/Remarks input anywhere, and the two popups are identical in field set (same form, keyed by whether an existingLead is being edited). (b) Therefore no Notes field label, placeholder, or validation rule exists to document — the scenario matches "no Notes field exists anywhere in the UI" rather than scenarios 1-3. (c) The backend fully supports notes end-to-end at the API/DB layer: leads.notes is in LEAD_FIELD_MAP so PATCH /leads/:id updates it whenever a "notes" key is present in the request body, POST /leads inserts body.notes on create, and serializeLead() includes notes in every GET/POST/PATCH response — so the value is even present in the frontend's in-memory Lead objects. The gap is purely in the two UI layers: the Add/Edit form never collects or sends a notes value, and Lead Detail/Leads-list never render the notes value that is already sitting in state. This means notes are currently write-only via direct database/seed access (or a raw API call bypassing the UI) and are never viewable or editable through any click-path in the product, for any role. (d) Add Comment / Activity Timeline on Lead Detail is a completely separate mechanism: comments POST to /leads/:id/activities and are stored as lead_activities rows (activity_type 'comment'), rendered in the Activity Timeline card alongside 'created', 'status_change', 'assignment', and 'follow_up' entries — they never read or write the leads.notes column. Confirmed via seed data that the "Ramesh Gupta" lead with status Converted (id ...0001) is assigned to Sneha Iyer (the demo Caller account), so the exact same seeded-notes record used to validate Admin behavior can also be used unmodified to validate Caller behavior. Test cases below document the confirmed absence of any Notes UI (Add Lead, Edit Lead, Lead Detail, Leads list), confirm editing/creating leads through the UI never surfaces or disturbs the hidden notes value, confirm Add Comment/Timeline is unrelated, verify role parity between Admin and Caller, and flag the apparent product gap for follow-up. No test case asks the tester to make raw API calls or inspect network traffic — all steps are plain browser clicks, consistent with a non-technical UAT tester.

## Lookup Data Sourcing & Dropdown Behavior — Assigned Caller, Lead Source, Dosage Form (Admin/Caller)

These cases probe how MediCRM's Add/Edit Lead, Add/Edit User, and Add/Edit Medicine modals actually source their dropdown options, verified against the current source: GET /api/lookups (server/src/routes/misc.ts) only serves leadStatuses, leadPriorities, leadSources, orderStages, paymentStatuses, followUpTypes and followUpStatuses — and critically, src/api/misc.ts's miscApi.lookups() is never called anywhere in the frontend (only miscApi.dashboard() is used in AppContext's loadAll), so it is dead code today. Lead Source options (src/pages/Leads.tsx leadSourceOptions) and Dosage Form options (src/pages/Medicines.tsx dosageFormOptions) are hardcoded arrays in the frontend, matching the DB seed values in db/migrations/002_enum_to_lookup.sql but fetched from neither /api/lookups nor any other endpoint. The 'Assigned Caller' dropdown is populated from state.users (loaded once via GET /api/users at login/HYDRATE) filtered client-side only by `u.role === 'caller'` — src/pages/Leads.tsx line 119 has NO `status === 'active'` filter, so deactivated callers are NOT excluded from the Assigned Caller dropdown; this is a real, confirmed gap versus what testers might expect. Because Add Lead/Add User/Add Medicine modals do not issue any network request when they open (all dropdown data is drawn from already-hydrated context state or hardcoded arrays), stopping the backend or blocking a request right as a modal opens has no visible effect on that modal — no spinner, no error state, no broken dropdown; a failure only surfaces if the user submits the form while the backend is unreachable, via api/client.ts's generic message "Unable to reach the server. Check your connection and try again." Medicine deactivation (isActive=false) is filtered out only in the Leads page's medicine-picker (`state.medicines.filter(m => m.isActive)` in Leads.tsx); the Medicines catalog page itself still lists inactive medicines, and any lead's medicine-required entries are stored as a denormalized name snapshot (lead_medicines.medicine_name) independent of the catalog item's later active/inactive state, so existing leads are unaffected either way. Seed data used below: Vikram Singh (EMP005, caller, active) and Kavya Reddy (EMP008, caller, already inactive) are both seeded callers; a seeded lead for customer "Suresh Rao" (mobile 9765432109) is already assigned to Vikram Singh, useful as a pre-existing assignment to test against deactivation. Testers should treat "the deactivated caller still appears in the dropdown" as the correct current behavior, not a false negative, unless product intentionally decides to change it.

Log in as Admin using aarav.sharma@medicrm.in / admin123.<br>2. Click 'User Management' in the left sidebar.<br>3. Verify the badge flips to a gray 'Inactive' badge immediately, with no confirmation prompt shown.<br>6. Click 'Leads' in the sidebar.<br>7. Click the 'Add Lead' button to open the Add New Lead modal.<br>8. This reflects that Leads.tsx builds the caller list via `state.users.filter(u => u.role === 'caller')` with no status filter. |

Log in as Admin through the normal login form.<br>2. If manually invoked, inspect the JSON response body. | The Network tab shows that /api/lookups is never called during normal navigation through Add Lead, Add User, or Add Medicine — confirming it is dead code (miscApi.lookups() defined in src/api/misc.ts is unused). If manually invoked, the endpoint responds with an object containing exactly the keys leadStatuses, leadPriorities, leadSources, orderStages, paymentStatuses, followUpTypes, followUpStatuses (each an array of {code, label} objects, active-only, sorted by sort_order) — it does not include callers or a dosage-form list at all, since those are not modeled as lookup tables in the schema. |

## Mobile & Responsive Layout — Sidebar, TopNav, Login, List Pages, Calendar, Modals

These cases cover mobile (375px) and tablet (768px) responsive behavior for the parts of MediCRM not already exercised by DASH-35/36/37, ORD-47, LEADS-53/MED-41, and the Calendar hover-tooltip cases. Findings are grounded in the current source: src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx, TopNav.tsx, Layout.tsx, src/pages/Login.tsx, Leads.tsx, Users.tsx, Medicines.tsx, Renewals.tsx, Calendar.tsx, and src/components/ui/Modal.tsx, PageHeader.tsx, Tabs.tsx, plus src/index.css (which imports Tailwind with no @theme breakpoint overrides, so the standard breakpoints apply: sm=640px, md=768px, lg=1024px). Key facts testers should know going in: (1) the sidebar's mobile/desktop split happens at the 1024px "lg" breakpoint, not at 768px — so the 768px tablet width is still in "mobile hamburger/off-canvas" mode, it is not a distinct squished tablet layout; (2) the TopNav global search box is fully removed from the DOM below 768px with no icon or overlay fallback — this is a genuine gap, not an oversight in this test plan; (3) list-page tables all share the same pattern (Card with overflow-hidden wrapping an inner overflow-x-auto div around the table), so they should scroll horizontally in place without moving the rest of the page; (4) the Calendar's monthly 7-column grid uses flexible (fr-unit) CSS grid tracks with no horizontal-scroll wrapper, so at narrow widths it is expected to squeeze columns very narrow rather than scroll or break; (5) two modals (Add/Edit User, Add/Edit Medicine) use a hard-coded two-column row for one form field pair with no mobile breakpoint, unlike the Add/Edit Lead modal which correctly collapses to one column below 640px — this asymmetry is called out explicitly rather than assumed away; (6) the Order detail modal's stage-progress stepper has no horizontal-scroll container of its own, unlike the medicines table directly below it in the same modal, which does. These were derived from static analysis of the current source (the local dev server could not be reached from this environment to visually confirm rendering), so testers should treat the "expected result" wording as the code's documented intent and flag any live-browser deviation as a bug in its own right.

## Keyboard Navigation & Accessibility

Covers keyboard-only operation and basic screen-reader friendliness across the app's forms, modals, and icon-only action buttons. Grounded in the actual current source (src/components/ui/Modal.tsx, Button.tsx, field-input/.field-label styles in src/index.css, and the Add/Edit form markup in Leads.tsx, Users.tsx, Medicines.tsx). Two concrete, code-verified gaps worth flagging up front rather than discovering by surprise: (1) every form field in the Add/Edit Lead, Add/Edit User, and Add/Edit Medicine modals renders its <label> and <input> as visually-adjacent siblings with no htmlFor/id pairing between them — a screen reader will not announce the field's label when the input receives focus, and clicking a label will not focus its input; this is present on every text field in all three modals, not an isolated case. (2) Modal.tsx closes on Escape and locks body scroll while open, but does not move focus into the dialog on open or trap Tab navigation within it — a keyboard user can Tab out of an open modal into the dimmed page behind it. Icon-only action buttons (View/Edit/Delete, Renew/Remind, etc.) generally do carry a native title attribute, which most browsers expose as an accessible name — verify this holds rather than assuming it from the title text alone.

| ID | Title | Role | Type | Priority | Preconditions | Steps | Expected Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A11Y-03 | Escape key closes an open modal | Both | Functional | P1 | Any modal open (Add/Edit Lead, User, Medicine, or the Order detail modal). | 1. With the modal open and no text field focused, press Escape. | The modal closes immediately, exactly as clicking Cancel or the X button would — this is explicitly wired in Modal.tsx's keydown handler. |
| A11Y-06 | Keyboard-only user can fully submit the Add Lead form without touching the mouse | Both | Functional | P1 | On the Leads page. | 1. Using only Tab/Shift+Tab/Enter/arrow keys/space, open Add Lead, fill every required field (typing into text fields, using arrow keys + Enter or typeahead for the native <select> dropdowns), and submit. | The lead is created successfully using keyboard input alone — native <select> elements and text inputs are all standard, keyboard-operable HTML controls, so this should work end-to-end without needing a mouse. |
| A11Y-01 | Tab order reaches every control in the Add Lead modal in a sensible sequence | Both | Edge Case | P2 | Logged in as either role, on the Leads page. | 1. Click "Add Lead" to open the modal.<br>2. Without using the mouse, press Tab repeatedly starting from the first field.<br>3. Continue through every field, the medicine picker, Add Another Medicine, Doctor Name, the three dropdowns, and finally Cancel/Add Lead. | Focus visits every interactive control exactly once, in the same top-to-bottom order the fields are laid out on screen, with a visible focus ring on each (per the .field-input:focus box-shadow rule) — no control is skipped and focus never silently jumps to something behind the modal. |
| A11Y-02 | Labels are not programmatically associated with their inputs (confirmed code gap) | Both | Edge Case | P2 | Any Add/Edit modal open (Lead, User, or Medicine), plus a screen reader (VoiceOver/NVDA) or the browser's Accessibility Inspector. | 1. Click directly on the text of a field label, e.g. "Customer Name".<br>2. Separately, Tab focus into that same field and check what a screen reader announces. | Clicking the label text does NOT move focus into the adjacent input (confirms no htmlFor/id pairing exists). A screen reader announces only the input's type/value, not the label text, when the field receives focus. This is a genuine, verified accessibility gap across all three modals — log it as a defect rather than a test failure of this plan. |
| A11Y-05 | Icon-only action buttons have an accessible name via their title attribute | Both | Edge Case | P2 | Leads or Users or Medicines list page, browser Accessibility Inspector open (or a screen reader). | 1. Inspect the View/Edit/Delete icon buttons on any row.<br>2. Check the computed accessible name for each in the browser's Accessibility panel. | Each icon-only button reports a non-empty accessible name matching its title attribute ("View", "Edit", "Delete") — confirm this holds for every icon-only button across Leads, Users, Medicines, and Renewals row actions, since a missing title on any one of them would make that button silent to a screen reader. |
| A11Y-07 | Sidebar and TopNav links are reachable and operable via keyboard | Both | Functional | P2 | Logged in, desktop viewport. | 1. From the page body, Tab through the sidebar navigation links.<br>2. Press Enter on a focused link (e.g. "Calendar"). | Each sidebar link shows a visible focus indicator as it's tabbed to, and pressing Enter navigates to that page — standard anchor/link behavior, no custom keydown handling interferes with it. |
| A11Y-04 | Tab does not trap focus inside an open modal (confirmed code gap) | Both | Edge Case | P3 | Any Add/Edit modal open. | 1. Tab forward past the modal's last control (e.g. past the "Add Lead" submit button).<br>2. Observe where focus lands. | Focus is NOT trapped inside the modal — it moves on to whatever element is next in the underlying page's DOM order (e.g. the dimmed table behind the overlay), which a keyboard-only user would find disorienting since the modal is still visually open and blocking the page. This is a confirmed gap (no focus trap implemented), included so the team can decide whether to prioritize a fix. |
| A11Y-08 | Notification bell dropdown is operable via keyboard | Both | Edge Case | P3 | Logged in as Sneha Iyer (has at least one notification). | 1. Tab to the bell icon button and press Enter/Space to open the dropdown.<br>2. Tab into the dropdown's notification rows and press Enter on one. | The dropdown opens via keyboard activation of the button (native <button> behavior), and each notification row (also a <button>) can be focused and activated with Enter, marking it read exactly as a mouse click would. |
| A11Y-09 | Required-field validation is announced or visually obvious for keyboard users | Both | Negative | P3 | On the Add Lead modal. | 1. Using keyboard only, attempt to submit the form with required fields left empty (Tab to the submit button and press Enter). | The browser's native HTML5 required-field validation intercepts submission and moves focus to (and highlights) the first empty required field, since these inputs use the `required` HTML attribute rather than custom JS validation — confirm this native behavior fires correctly rather than the form silently failing to submit with no indication why. |


## 7. Sign-off Checklist

Before considering MediCRM ready for release, confirm:

- [ ] All **P0** cases (183 total) pass for both Admin and Caller roles where applicable.
- [ ] All **P1** cases (253 total) pass, or any failures are explicitly accepted/deferred by the product owner.
- [ ] Every item in the **Permissions Matrix** (Section 4) has been individually verified — not just spot-checked — since the UI does not visually distinguish most Admin-only actions for a Caller.
- [ ] Every item in **Known Limitations** (Section 5) has been reviewed with the product owner to confirm each is still acceptable to ship as-is, especially:
  - [ ] The Caller self-delete-account risk (no confirmation, no recovery path).
  - [ ] The lack of optimistic-concurrency protection on Leads/Users/Medicines edits.
  - [ ] The completely inert "Notes" field on leads (collected and displayed nowhere, despite full backend support).
- [ ] No P0/P1 defect newly discovered during this pass remains unresolved or untriaged.
- [ ] The database has been re-seeded (or an equivalent clean state restored) after this test pass, if it will be reused for a demo or another test cycle.

**Sign-off:**

| Role | Name | Date | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tester | | | ☐ Pass ☐ Pass with noted exceptions ☐ Fail |
| Product Owner | | | ☐ Approved ☐ Approved with conditions ☐ Not approved |
